The spherical triangles, lunes, and doubles of Sections 6.7 and 6.8 keep their residuals of this section bit for bit (DOM-30).

DOM-30. The stored values of a reflection domain and the residuals of its checks MUST NOT depend on the code that builds and checks the doubles, the polygons, the wallpaper domains, and the spherical group domains, so that every figure of Sections 8.4 and 9.3 to 9.5 for a reflection domain holds bit for bit, because the golden images of `escher-render` compare reflection domains byte for byte, and the generating domain of each double must equal the reflection domain of its starred signature. The test "Generating domain" of Section 12.1 checks the second part, and the tests of the reflection domains, which print the figures of these sections, check the first. On the prototype outside the repository, a copy of this crate with the polygon added kept every unit test, integration test, and doctest of the triangles and the kites passing, which is evidence for this rule given DOM-46 (Section 14).

The code of three and four vertices, of the Euclidean domains, and of the spherical domains does not depend on the measurement of DOM-46 for five or more vertices. A program outside the repository compared the raw bits, by `f64::to_bits`, of every value of `corner_angles()`, of `area()`, and of the six residuals of `verify()` of 1610 domains, built by the crate with and without that measurement, and found them identical. The domains are every reflection triangle and kite over the orders 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 20, 50, 100, 500, 999, and 1000, `*n22`, `n22`, `*nn`, and `nn` for $n$ from 2 to 60, and the Euclidean and spherical signatures with their doubles. The same comparison of the same 1610 domains, with and without the double-double check 4 of DOM-49 and the kinds of step 5 of Section 6.8, found every stored value, kind, and residual identical. Over the 5204 signatures of the named and the small polygons and their rotation signatures, it found the geometry, the angles, the area, and checks 1, 2, 3, 5, and 6 identical. No committed test repeats the comparison, because no test compares the angles, the area, or a residual of one of these 1610 domains or 5204 signatures bit for bit with a stored value (Section 12.1), and the last bits of the mathematical library can differ between the container and the runner of CI.

## 9. Numerical Robustness

### 9.1. Renormalization

- `hyperbolic::normalize_point` runs twice, in steps 6 and 10 of Section 6.1, each time on a sum of vertices.
- `hyperbolic::wall_through` divides each normal by its norm, and `euclidean::wall_through` divides each covector by the length of its first two coordinates (the geometry design).
- The Euclidean construction divides a sum of vertices by its last coordinate in steps 3 and 5 of Section 6.5.
- `spherical::normalize_point` runs twice for a spherical triangle, in steps 4 and 6 of Section 6.7, each time on a sum of vertices, and `spherical::wall_through` divides each normal by its length. The lune renormalizes nothing.
- The double of Section 6.8 renormalizes nothing. Its image vertices and normals are plain products of $R_1$ and the values of its generating domain.
- The polygon of Section 6.10 normalizes the sum of its vertices once in double-double arithmetic, in step 8, and once in `f64` for the centre, in step 10, and `hyperbolic::wall_through` divides each normal by its norm. It renormalizes no vertex.
- `verify()` renormalizes nothing (DOM-8).

DOM-10. The construction MUST NOT renormalize the vertices after step 8, after step 4 of Section 6.5, in step 2 of Section 6.8, or after step 9 of Section 6.10, because a renormalized vertex lies on the hyperboloid but far from the true corner. Section 14 records the figures for $(1000, 1000, 1000)$, which no measurement of the code covers.

A vertex therefore leaves the hyperboloid by a small amount. Over the triples of the reference sweep of Section 9.3, $\lvert \langle V, V \rangle + 1 \rvert$ reaches $4.113 \times 10^{-5}$, at $(995, 987, 976)$. The wall through two vertices depends only on the plane that they span, so the normals are not affected.

### 9.2. Forms

- `hyperbolic::inverse` computes the geometric inverse $J M^t J$ (Section 6.1 of the geometry reference), never a general matrix inverse.
- The relators are evaluated in the corner frame (Section 8.2, check 4).
- The corner angles come from the distances between the vertices (Section 7).
- $\operatorname{arcosh} X$ in step 4 needs no special form. The smallest $X$ that the construction computes over the triples of the reference sweep of Section 9.3 is 1.040349, at `*732`.
- $\operatorname{arcosh} X$ and $\operatorname{arsinh} X$ in step 5 of Section 6.10 need no special form over the range of Section 6.6, on the smallest arguments that the prototype outside the repository found (DOM-41). A gap that is not finite or not positive gives `Numerical` in step 12.
- The walk and the placement of a polygon run in double-double arithmetic (DOM-42), and the angles of five or more vertices come from `atan2` in the corner frame (DOM-46).
- $\arccos X$ in step 2 of the spherical triangle is clamped to $[-1, 1]$. For `*n22` with a large $n$, $X_b$ comes close to 1, where the arccosine is ill-conditioned, and $\cos(\pi/2)$ is not 0 in `f64`, so Section 9.5 measures the triangle up to $n = 1000$.

### 9.3. Accuracy over the Supported Range

DOM-11. The values that `build_with` returns MUST lie within the bounds of this table, or for a Euclidean domain of Section 9.4, for a spherical domain of Section 9.5, for a polygon and its double of Section 9.7, for one of the ten domains of Section 6.13, of Section 9.8, and for a double also of Section 9.6, of the values of the exact polygon in the final position, because the renderer and `escher info` use them without a check, and the reference-arithmetic tests of Section 12.1 assert these bounds.

The rows for the side length, the corner angle, and the area bound the values that `side_lengths()`, `corner_angles()`, and `area()` return, which the crate measures from the stored vertices (Section 7), with lengths and areas at curvature $-1$ and angles in radians.

| Quantity | Measure | Orders up to 12 | $(1000, 3, 2)$ | Every supported triple |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Largest coordinate error divided by the largest coordinate | $10^{-13}$ | $10^{-11}$ | $10^{-8}$ |
| Normal | Largest coordinate error | $10^{-13}$ | $10^{-11}$ | $10^{-8}$ |
| Side length | Absolute error | $10^{-13}$ | $10^{-11}$ | $10^{-8}$ |
| Centre | Largest coordinate distance from `hyperbolic::ORIGIN` | $10^{-13}$ | $10^{-11}$ | $10^{-9}$ |
| Corner angle | Absolute error | $10^{-9}$ | $10^{-9}$ | $10^{-9}$ |
| Area | Absolute error | $10^{-9}$ | $10^{-9}$ | $10^{-9}$ |

The bounds are choices of this design. The test "Triangles" of Section 12.1 measures each quantity against the reference arithmetic of Section 12.2, asserts each bound, and prints the largest error with its triple and its margin. The test "Third side" measures the distance from vertex 0 to vertex 1, which the construction never sets, against the bounds of the side length. The error of the area is measured against the area of the reference triangle. The column for orders up to 12 covers all 269 hyperbolic triples with orders up to 12. The column for every supported triple covers the 294 triples of the set of Section 12.1, which are those 269 and the 25 named triples with an order above 12. The margin is the bound divided by the largest error. Every margin is at least 8, as TEST-13 of the coding standard requires:

| Quantity | Orders up to 12: largest error, where, margin | $(1000, 3, 2)$: largest error, margin | Set of 294 triples: largest error, where, margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | $9.104 \times 10^{-15}$, $(11, 10, 10)$, 11.0 | $1.241 \times 10^{-14}$, 806 | $1.573 \times 10^{-10}$, $(997, 931, 906)$, 63.6 |
| Normal | $1.139 \times 10^{-14}$, $(11, 10, 10)$, 8.8 | $5.932 \times 10^{-15}$, 1686 | $1.806 \times 10^{-10}$, $(998, 998, 958)$, 55.4 |
| Side length | $1.233 \times 10^{-14}$, $(11, 10, 10)$, 8.1 | $1.723 \times 10^{-14}$, 580 | $2.016 \times 10^{-10}$, $(978, 977, 971)$, 49.6 |
| Centre | $3.057 \times 10^{-15}$, $(12, 9, 8)$, 32.7 | $2.872 \times 10^{-15}$, 3482 | $1.018 \times 10^{-10}$, $(973, 972, 964)$, 9.8 |
| Corner angle | $2.985 \times 10^{-15}$, $(11, 10, 10)$, $3.35 \times 10^{5}$ | $8.564 \times 10^{-14}$, $1.17 \times 10^{4}$ | $5.496 \times 10^{-13}$, $(998, 998, 958)$, 1820 |
| Area | $4.655 \times 10^{-15}$, $(12, 8, 8)$, $2.15 \times 10^{5}$ | $6.864 \times 10^{-14}$, $1.46 \times 10^{4}$ | $9.732 \times 10^{-13}$, $(997, 931, 906)$, 1028 |
| Third side | $1.233 \times 10^{-14}$, $(11, 10, 10)$, 8.1 | Not measured separately | $2.016 \times 10^{-10}$, $(978, 977, 971)$, 49.6 |

Two sweeps outside the repository extend these figures, and no test in the repository repeats them:

- The reference sweep compares every quantity of the table with the same reference arithmetic on 63,123 distinct triples, and finds no error above the figures of the set of 294 triples. Its largest vertex error is $1.544 \times 10^{-10}$, at $(992, 991, 975)$, and its largest normal error $1.625 \times 10^{-10}$, at $(989, 976, 974)$. Its triples are the union of five sets:
  - every hyperbolic triple with orders up to 30
  - every triple with all three orders from 970 to 1000
  - every triple with $n_0$ from 900 to 1000 and $n_1$ and $n_2$ up to 30
  - every hyperbolic triple of orders from the grid $\{2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12, 15, 20, 25, 30, 40, 50, 75, 100, 150, 200, 250, 300, 400, 500, 600, 700, 800, 900, 950, 1000\}$
  - the families $(n, 3, 2)$, $(n, 4, 4)$, $(n, n, 2)$, $(n, n, n)$, $(n, 4, 3)$, and $(n, 7, 6)$ for $n$ up to 1000
- The sweep of Section 8.4 over every supported triple needs no reference for the centre, whose exact value is `hyperbolic::ORIGIN`. Its largest centre offset is the figure of the set of 294 triples, $1.018 \times 10^{-10}$ at $(973, 972, 964)$. It measures the angles and the area only through checks 5 and 6, which compare with $\pi/n_j$ and with the area of the signature that the domain stores (Section 8.2).

No measurement covers the vertices, normals, and side lengths of every supported triple. The column "Every supported triple" bounds them on the strength of the set of 294 triples and the reference sweep.

Over the triples of the reference sweep, the vertices of this crate lie up to distance 6.600 from `hyperbolic::ORIGIN`, at $(1000, 1000, 1000)$, and Section 9.1 states how far they leave the hyperboloid. Section 8.4 of the geometry design states that every result of `hyperbolic::side`, `hyperbolic::distance`, `hyperbolic::wall_through`, `hyperbolic::translation`, and `hyperbolic::reflection` on these vertices and the centre was within the bound of its row, although some of these inputs lie outside the ranges of the rows. It states that its tests do not measure `hyperbolic::normalize_point` on the sums of vertices of steps 6 and 10, or `hyperbolic::isometry_defect` on the pairings. The centre and check 1 therefore rest only on the end-to-end measurements of this section and of Section 8.4, and the bounds above keep the end-to-end figures and margins for every quantity.

### 9.4. Accuracy of the Euclidean Domains

The Euclidean domains of the tests are those of `*632`, `*442`, and `*333`, and the rectangle of `*2222` at `aspect` 1, 1.618, 4, 0.125, and 8, and at 64 values spaced logarithmically from 0.125 to 8. Over these domains, the test "Euclidean domains" of Section 12.1 compares each quantity of this table except the last with the reference construction of Section 12.2, asserts each bound, and prints the largest error with its domain and its margin. The 72 domains have 285 sides. The bounds are choices of this design, and every margin is at least 8, as TEST-13 of the coding standard requires:

| Quantity | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Largest coordinate error | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.290 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*2222` at `aspect` 0.17388 | 15.5 |
| Covector | Largest coordinate error | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.290 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*2222` at `aspect` 0.17388 | 15.5 |
| Pairing | Largest entry error | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.581 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*2222` at `aspect` 0.17388 | 15.5 |
| Side length | Absolute error | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.581 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*2222` at `aspect` 0.17388 | 15.5 |
| Corner angle | Absolute error | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.684 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*632` | 11.9 |
| Area | Absolute error, against the area of the reference polygon | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.110 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*2222` at `aspect` 0.15238 | 18.0 |
| Centre | Largest coordinate distance from `euclidean::ORIGIN` | $10^{-15}$ | $5.551 \times 10^{-17}$ | `*632` | 18.0 |
| Aspect of the rectangle | Relative error of the length of side 0 divided by that of side 1, against `aspect` | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.220 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*2222` at `aspect` 1.618 | 18.0 |
| SymmHub lines | Largest coordinate error of each wall of a triangle against its line of SymmHub, up to sign (Section 12.1) | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.110 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 2 of `*333` | 18.0 |

The test "SymmHub lines" measures the last row, and the three lines of SymmHub match sides 0, 2, and 1, in that order, for each of the three triangles.

At `aspect` 1 and 4, every number of the construction of the rectangle is a dyadic rational and every operation is exact in IEEE 754 arithmetic, and the square roots of 1 and 4 are exact. The test "Exact rectangle" therefore asserts exactly that the vertices are $(\pm w/2, \pm h/2, 1)$ and the covectors are $(\pm 1, 0, -w/2)$ and $(0, \pm 1, -h/2)$, as TEST-13 of the coding standard allows.

DOM-19. A test that asserts a bound on a quantity of a domain MUST name its input set and print its largest value with its input and its margin, as TEST-13 of the coding standard requires. This document MUST record a bound only where it is part of a contract of the crate, because the test repeats the measurement on every run, while a figure copied into this document goes stale with the next change to the code.

### 9.5. Accuracy of the Spherical Domains

The spherical domains of the tests are those of `*332`, `*432`, `*532`, and `*222`, and of `*n22` and `*nn` for $n$ = 2, 3, 5, 12, 100, 999, and 1000. Over these domains, the test "Spherical domains" of Section 12.1 compares each quantity of this table with the reference construction of Section 12.2, asserts each bound, and prints the largest error with its domain and its margin. The test builds `*222` twice, as `*222` and as `*n22` with $n = 2$, so it measures 18 domains with 47 sides. The bounds are choices of this design, set from the largest errors measured, and every margin is at least 8, as TEST-13 of the coding standard and DOM-19 require:

| Quantity | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Largest coordinate error | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $2.785 \times 10^{-14}$ | `*(1000)22` | 14.4 |
| Normal | Largest coordinate error | $10^{-13}$ | $7.764 \times 10^{-15}$ | `*(999)22` | 12.9 |
| Side length | Absolute error | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $3.892 \times 10^{-14}$ | `*(999)22` | 10.3 |
| Centre | Largest coordinate error against the reference centre | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.241 \times 10^{-16}$ | `*(1000)22` | 16.1 |
| Corner angle | Absolute error against $\pi/n_j$ | $2 \times 10^{-13}$ | $2.038 \times 10^{-14}$ | `*(1000)22` | 9.8 |
| Area | Absolute error against $2\pi\chi$ | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $4.075 \times 10^{-14}$ | `*(1000)(1000)` | 9.8 |

The vertices and the centre of a lune are exact values that the construction stores, and the dot product of its two vertices is exactly $-1$. The test asserts these equalities exactly, as TEST-13 of the coding standard allows, and compares the side lengths of a lune with $\pi$ within the bound of the side length.

### 9.6. Accuracy of the Rotation Domains

The vertices, normals, and covectors that a double takes from its generating domain are those of Sections 9.3 to 9.5 bit for bit (DOM-30), and so is its centre. This section bounds what the doubling adds: the image vertices, normals, and covectors, the pairings of DOM-25, and the measurements of Section 7.

The doubles of the tests are those of the cone triples of the set of 294 triples of Section 12.1, of `632`, `442`, `333`, and of `2222` at the 69 values of `aspect` of Section 9.4, and of `332`, `432`, `532`, `222`, and of `n22` and `nn` for $n$ = 2, 3, 5, 12, 100, 999, and 1000. The test "Rotation domains" of Section 12.1 compares each quantity of this table with the reference of Section 12.2, which builds the double itself, asserts each bound, and prints the largest error with its domain and its margin. The test "Compensated pairings" measures the rows of the pairing and of the isometry defect over the same doubles. The bounds are choices of this design, each the smallest value of the form 1, 2, or 4 times a power of 10 that is at least 8 times the largest error measured, except the area bound of the spherical doubles, which is the area bound $4 \times 10^{-13}$ of Section 9.5. Every margin is at least 8, as TEST-13 of the coding standard and DOM-19 require. The hyperbolic kites are the 294 doubles of the cone triples, the Euclidean doubles are 72, and the spherical doubles 18:

| Quantity | Measure | Hyperbolic kites: bound, largest error, where, margin | Euclidean doubles: bound, largest error, where, margin | Spherical doubles: bound, largest error, where, margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Image vertex | Largest coordinate error, relative to the largest coordinate for a hyperbolic domain | $4 \times 10^{-9}$, $3.234 \times 10^{-10}$, `(998)(998)(958)`, 12.4 | $10^{-14}$, $5.187 \times 10^{-16}$, `2222` at `aspect` 5.0397, 19.3 | $4 \times 10^{-13}$, $2.686 \times 10^{-14}$, `(999)22`, 14.9 |
| Image normal or covector | Largest coordinate error of the normal of a hyperbolic or spherical double, and of the covector of a Euclidean double | $10^{-8}$, $6.223 \times 10^{-10}$, `(995)(983)(970)`, 16.1 | $10^{-14}$, $5.187 \times 10^{-16}$, `2222` at `aspect` 5.0397, 19.3 | $2 \times 10^{-13}$, $2.329 \times 10^{-14}$, `(999)22`, 8.6 |
| Pairing | Largest entry error against the product, in the reference arithmetic, of the two reflections that the domain stores | $2 \times 10^{-13}$, $1.363 \times 10^{-14}$, side 2 of `(923)65`, 14.7 | $10^{-15}$, $5.794 \times 10^{-17}$, side 0 of `333`, 17.3 | $10^{-15}$, $5.485 \times 10^{-17}$, side 2 of `222`, 18.2 |
| Isometry defect of a pairing | The value of the isometry check of the geometry | $2 \times 10^{-10}$, $1.819 \times 10^{-11}$, side 2 of `(928)43`, 11.0 | $4 \times 10^{-15}$, $4.441 \times 10^{-16}$, side 0 of `632`, 9.0 | $2 \times 10^{-14}$, $1.443 \times 10^{-15}$, side 2 of `522`, 13.9 |
| Side length | Absolute error | $2 \times 10^{-9}$, $1.444 \times 10^{-10}$, `(985)(985)(971)`, 13.8 | $10^{-14}$, $6.419 \times 10^{-16}$, `2222` at `aspect` 5.0397, 15.6 | $4 \times 10^{-13}$, $3.914 \times 10^{-14}$, `(1000)22`, 10.2 |
| Corner angle | Absolute error against the copies times $\pi$ divided by the order | $10^{-11}$, $9.090 \times 10^{-13}$, `(978)(977)(971)`, 11.0 | $4 \times 10^{-15}$, $3.369 \times 10^{-16}$, `333`, 11.9 | $10^{-12}$, $7.073 \times 10^{-14}$, `(1000)22`, 14.1 |
| Flat vertex | Absolute error of the angle against $\pi$ | Not applicable, because no hyperbolic kite has a flat vertex | $10^{-15}$, $1.225 \times 10^{-16}$, `2222` at `aspect` 1, 8.2 | $10^{-12}$, $7.073 \times 10^{-14}$, `(1000)22`, 14.1 |
| Area | Absolute error against the area of the signature, or for a Euclidean double against the area of the reference polygon | $2 \times 10^{-11}$, $1.946 \times 10^{-12}$, `(997)(931)(906)`, 10.3 | $2 \times 10^{-15}$, $2.220 \times 10^{-16}$, `2222` at `aspect` 0.21197, 9.0 | $4 \times 10^{-13}$, $4.938 \times 10^{-15}$, `(1000)22`, 81.0 |

The test asserts its smallest numbers of samples. For the hyperbolic kites, these are 294 image vertices, 588 image normals, and 1176 sides. For the Euclidean doubles, they are 141 image vertices, 213 image covectors, 426 sides, and 138 flat vertices. For the spherical doubles, they are 11 image vertices, 29 image normals, 58 sides, and 12 flat vertices. The row of the image normal or covector compares the covector of a Euclidean double, because its normal is derived from its covector (step 3 of Section 6.8). The error of a flat vertex of a Euclidean double is measured against the exact $\pi$, so it includes the difference between the `f64` value of $\pi$ and $\pi$.

The pairing row compares with the product of the two `f64` reflections that the domain stores, because DOM-25 computes that product and not the product of the reflections of the reference. The test "Compensated pairings" also asserts that every entry of every pairing is that exact product rounded to the nearest `f64`, and prints how many entries differ from it, for the compensated and for the plain product:

| Doubles | Entries | Entries that differ, compensated product | Entries that differ, plain product |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Hyperbolic kites | 10,584 | 0 | 4,050 |
| Euclidean doubles | 3,834 | 0 | 9 |
| Spherical doubles | 522 | 0 | 160 |

The isometry row is the defect that remains with the compensated product. Measurements that no test of the crate repeats compare the largest isometry defect of the pairings of a double with the compensated and with the plain product, for these doubles:

| Double | Compensated product | Plain product |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `(1000)32` | $9.095 \times 10^{-13}$ | $2.728 \times 10^{-12}$ |
| `(928)43` | $1.819 \times 10^{-11}$ | $1.819 \times 10^{-11}$ |
| `(868)44` | $1.455 \times 10^{-11}$ | $1.091 \times 10^{-11}$ |
| `(998)44` | $7.276 \times 10^{-12}$ | $1.455 \times 10^{-11}$ |
| `(923)65` | $7.276 \times 10^{-12}$ | $1.091 \times 10^{-11}$ |
| `(1000)(1000)2` | $2.220 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.332 \times 10^{-15}$ |
| `(973)(972)(964)` | $7.105 \times 10^{-15}$ | $5.329 \times 10^{-15}$ |

Over the cone triples of the 30 named triples, the largest defect is $1.819 \times 10^{-11}$, at `(928)43`, for both products. At `(1000)32`, the defect of the compensated product equals that of the reflections that the domain stores. The compensated product therefore lowers the defect at some doubles and raises it at others, and does not lower the largest defect over those triples. The test "Compensated pairings" bounds the defect of the compensated product, which the construction uses, and with plain products it would fail on the entries that differ.

### 9.7. Accuracy of the Polygons and Their Doubles

The tests of the polygons use these sets. The module `shared` defines the named and the small polygons, so that the unit tests use them too:

- The named polygons: `*3222`, `*3322`, `*3333`, `*4444`, `*5432`, `*99994`, `*22222`, `*33333`, `*222222`, `*444444`, `*2222222`, `*8765432`, `*22222222`, `*33333333`, `*44444444`, `*(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)`, `*(12)(12)333333`, `*(1000)222`, `*(1000)2222`, `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)`, `*(1000)2222222`, and `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)`.
- The small polygons: every canonical hyperbolic signature of one boundary with four corners of orders from 2 to 7, five corners of orders from 2 to 7, six of orders from 2 to 6, seven of orders from 2 to 5, or eight of orders from 2 to 4. The port of Section 6.8 of `docs/research/symmhub.md` ran these orders as 47,641 corner sequences as typed. The prototype of Section 14 reduced them to 4,421 canonical signatures. The test uses the canonical signatures, and it asserts and prints its own count.
- The settings of a polygon: the default shape, each parameter alone at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, and 3.5 with the others at their defaults, and 32 settings drawn uniformly from the range with a fixed seed. Each test draws them with a seed of its own, so the numbers of refused settings differ between the tests.
- The doubles of polygons: the rotation signatures of the named polygons and of the small polygons, at the default shape. Every one of them builds on the machine of the measurement. The double of `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` is the one double whose smallest margin is below 2, which the tests accept as built or refused (Section 8.4).
- The default sweep of the prototype of Section 14: the 9049 canonical signatures with four corners of orders from 2 to 12, five of orders from 2 to 9, six of orders from 2 to 6, seven of orders from 2 to 5, or eight of orders from 2 to 4, and every signature of four to eight corners with at least one corner of order 1000 and the other orders from $\{2, 3, 7\}$. No committed test runs it. A program outside the repository ran it with the code of this crate. The counts of signatures with a corner of order 1000 in the table of the default sweep below come from that program. This definition does not reproduce them: with four to six corners, it gives 18, 45, and 135 signatures, and the program counted 28, 73, and 208. The set that a test of the crate measures with four to six corners and a corner of order 1000 is that of Section 8.4, with 34, 97, and 338 signatures.

The test "Polygons" of Section 12.1 compares each quantity of this table other than the closing side with the reference of Section 12.2, over the named polygons at each of their settings, asserts each bound, and prints the largest error with its signature, its setting, and its margin. It also bounds the 17 named polygons whose orders are all at most 12, at their default shapes, in a column of their own, because the bounds over all settings are set by polygons with a corner of order 1000, and so would hide a loss of accuracy on ordinary polygons. The test "Rotation polygons" does the same for the 21 doubles of the named polygons at their default shapes other than the double at the limit, `(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)`, which may build or be refused (Section 8.4), so that its bounds do not depend on that outcome. A setting that the guard refuses is counted and asserted to give `Numerical`, and contributes no error. Each bound is the smallest value of the form 1, 2, or 4 times a power of 10 that is at least 8 times the largest error measured, as TEST-13 of the coding standard and DOM-19 require. The settings of "Polygons" come from the seed `0x6a09_e667_f3bc_c908`. Of them, 976 build and the guard refuses 70, each with `Numerical` and the text of the guard. The table gives each bound, the largest error, its place, its margin, and the number of samples:

Largest error $1.017 \times 10^{-14}$, at `*44444444`, a margin of 9.8, over 17 polygons | Bound $10^{-11}$. Largest error $9.511 \times 10^{-13}$, at `44444444`, a margin of 10.5, over 21 doubles |

The double at the limit builds on the machine of the measurement. The test "Rotation polygons" prints its largest errors and bounds none of them: $3.547 \times 10^{-14}$ for the vertex, $8.792 \times 10^{-12}$ for the normal, $2.716 \times 10^{-13}$ for the side length, $1.849 \times 10^{-17}$ for the centre, $9.629 \times 10^{-14}$ for the corner angle, and $1.438 \times 10^{-13}$ for the area. It has no flat vertex.

The test "Polygons" also asserts the identities of DOM-48 in the reference, within $10^{-50}$. The angles measured from the reference vertices by the law of cosines equal $\pi/n_j$, with the largest error $7.124 \times 10^{-58}$ at `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)`, a margin of $1.4 \times 10^{7}$, over 5963 angles. $(n - 2)\pi$ minus their sum equals $-2\pi\chi$ of `escher-signature`, with the largest error $1.477 \times 10^{-57}$ at the same polygon, a margin of $6.8 \times 10^{6}$, over 976 polygons.

The residual of check 5 is at most the largest error of the corner angle, and the 106-bit simulation of the walk gave the residuals of check 5 that DOM-42 states. The centres of the doubles of the crate lie within $7.080 \times 10^{-17}$ of the origin, where those of the prototype, with the `f64` walk, lay within $9.9 \times 10^{-14}$. With the `f64` walk, the prototype also gave the angles at the vertices with the copies 2 within $6.4 \times 10^{-11}$ of their expected values in the corner frame.

The test "Canonical input" compares two builds of the crate, not a build with the reference, so it uses its own relative vertex bound $4 \times 10^{-15}$, doubled, and not the vertex bound of the table. That bound is at least 8 times the relative vertex error of $2.64 \times 10^{-16}$ that a program outside the repository measured against a polygon of 60 digits, on `*3322`, `*3222`, `*5432`, and `*33222`.

The test "Double-double functions" of Section 12.1 compares the functions of DOM-42 with the reference of Section 12.2 over the lengths from 0 to 30 in steps of $1/64$ and over $\pi/n$ for $n$ from 2 to 1000. It measures $\cos$ and $\sin$ in absolute terms, because $\cos(\pi/2)$ is 0. Each bound follows DOM-19:

| Function | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Units of $2^{-106}$ | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $\cosh$ | Relative | $2 \times 10^{-30}$ | $1.210 \times 10^{-31}$ | 9.81 | Length 22.609375 | 16.5 |
| $\sinh$ | Relative | $2 \times 10^{-30}$ | $1.298 \times 10^{-31}$ | 10.53 | Length 22.828125 | 15.4 |
| $\cos(\pi/n)$ | Absolute | $2 \times 10^{-31}$ | $1.874 \times 10^{-32}$ | 1.52 | $\pi/337$ | 10.7 |
| $\sin(\pi/n)$ | Absolute | $2 \times 10^{-31}$ | $1.265 \times 10^{-32}$ | 1.03 | $\pi/5$ | 15.8 |

The test also asserts that the double-double $\pi$ lies within 2 units of $2^{-106}$ of the reference $\pi$, and that the sum, the product, the quotient, and the square root are exact on representable results, such as $(1 + 2^{-30})^2$ with its quotient and its root. The error of about 10 units in $\cosh$ and $\sinh$ comes from the reduction by $k \ln 2$, with a double-double $\ln 2$ and $k$ up to 44, and from the 10 squarings.

The test "Length parameters" of Section 12.1 compares the value of each length parameter with the length that it names on the built polygon. The unit part of the test "Default shape" asserts that the radius $d$ of the bisection of DOM-45 gives $\lvert g(d) - \pi \rvert$ within its bound, over the named and the small polygons. Each bound follows DOM-19:

| Quantity | Bound | Largest error | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A length parameter against the length that it names, absolute | $4 \times 10^{-14}$ | $4.885 \times 10^{-15}$ | 8.2 |
| $\lvert g(d) - \pi \rvert$ at the radius of the bisection | $2 \times 10^{-14}$ | $2.220 \times 10^{-15}$ | 9.0 |

The tests "Cut meaning" and "Walk against weld", and the reference part of "Default shape", assert the identities of DOM-48 in the reference arithmetic of Section 12.2. The last row compares the defaults of the crate with the reference:

| Test | Identity | Tolerance | Largest error | Where | Samples |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Cut meaning | The side of a corner pair against its parameter | $10^{-50}$ | $2.366 \times 10^{-66}$ | `*(1000)2222222` with the setting $(2.5648, 2.5044, 0.9578, 1.4838, 0.6277)$ | 995 |
| Cut meaning | The distance between the walls of a slice against its length | $10^{-50}$ | $5.200 \times 10^{-55}$ | `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` with the setting $(2.5494, 1.7481, 1.3667, 1.7175, 0.5177)$ | 2357 |
| Walk against weld | The distance between two vertices, welded layout against walk | $10^{-50}$ | $2.789 \times 10^{-58}$ | Vertices 5 and 6 of `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` | 350 pairs |
| Walk against weld | The angle at an end of a welded slice against $\pi$ | $10^{-50}$ | $4.362 \times 10^{-69}$ | `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` | 88 ends of 44 slices |
| Walk against weld | The closure of each laid-out sheet, as the largest entry of its frame minus the identity | $10^{-50}$ | $7.187 \times 10^{-66}$ | `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` | 66 sheets |
| Default shape | The angle of the inscribed polygon against $\pi/n_j$ | $10^{-60}$ | $1.821 \times 10^{-70}$ | Vertex 6 of `*5453452` | 253 |
| Default shape | The distance of each side of the inscribed polygon from the centre against the inradius | $10^{-60}$ | $2.177 \times 10^{-70}$ | Side 0 of `*44444444` | 253 |
| Default shape | $\operatorname{arcosh} \lvert \langle u_a, u_b \rangle \rvert$ of a slice against the distance between its walls by search | $10^{-50}$ | $1.348 \times 10^{-70}$ | Slice 2 of `*(1000)2222222` | 97 |
| Default shape | A default of the crate against its unrounded reference value | $5 \times 10^{-4} + 10^{-12}$ | $5.000 \times 10^{-4}$ less about $9.3 \times 10^{-9}$ | `slice1.length` of `*422322` | 127 |

"Cut meaning" runs on the named polygons at all their settings, including those that the guard refuses, because it runs in the reference alone. It confirms the meaning of a slice length of Section 6.6 (`UNV-7` and `UNV-27`) to $5.2 \times 10^{-55}$ at 256 bits, where the prototype reached $7 \times 10^{-27}$ at 50 digits (Section 14). "Walk against weld" runs on the 22 named polygons at their default shapes. The reference part of "Default shape" runs on the 22 named polygons, 18 of the small polygons, and `*422322` and `*432232`, 42 polygons in all (Section 12.1). Every unrounded reference default of these lies strictly inside 0.5 to 3.5, so none is clamped. The test prints the smallest distance of an unrounded reference default from a boundary of the rounding: $9.255 \times 10^{-9}$, at `slice1.length` of `*422322`, over 127 defaults. An error of the unrounded default of the crate above about $9.3 \times 10^{-9}$ flips the rounding of that default, which then lies $5 \times 10^{-4} + 9.3 \times 10^{-9}$ from its exact value and fails the allowance. The test therefore detects such an error. The largest error of the last row, a margin of about 1.0, comes from the rounding at that default. It does not mean that the crate is near the allowance, because a correctly rounded default always lies within $5 \times 10^{-4}$ less its distance from the boundary.

A program outside the repository compared the stored vertices of the crate with a walk at 60 digits of the same `f64` side lengths, placed the same way. On `*3333`, `*(1000)222`, `*(1000)2222`, `*(1000)323222`, `*(1000)2222222`, and `*(1000)3232223`, the largest difference of a coordinate, relative to the time coordinate of its vertex, was 0.55 to 0.90 units of $2^{-53}$. The double-double walk is therefore within one unit of the last place of the exact walk of the same lengths, and its arithmetic does not limit the accuracy of the polygons.

The `f64` side lengths of step 6 do move individual residuals. The same program built the exact polygon both from the `f64` side lengths of the crate and from exact side lengths, and its vertices differ from those of exact lengths by up to $7.5 \times 10^{-15}$ relative:

| Signature | Residual of check 1, crate | Exact walk of the `f64` lengths of the crate | Exact polygon of exact lengths |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `*(1000)2222` | $2.9 \times 10^{-11}$ | $2.9 \times 10^{-11}$ | $3.6 \times 10^{-12}$ |
| `*(1000)2222222` | $1.7 \times 10^{-10}$ | $1.7 \times 10^{-10}$ | $5.8 \times 10^{-11}$ |
| `*(1000)3232223` | $2.9 \times 10^{-10}$ | $2.9 \times 10^{-10}$ | $1.7 \times 10^{-10}$ |

Over the default sweep, the share of margins below 8 stays about the same with exact lengths, as the next table shows, so the prototype's reading of the `f64` lengths as harmless holds over the sweep and not signature by signature.

The same program built the default shape of every signature of the default sweep with the code of this crate. The guard refuses none. The binding check is always check 1 or check 2. The last two columns come from the prototype's own exact construction in mpmath, at the rounded defaults of the crate, with its own copy of `verify()`:

| Corners | Orders | Signatures | Smallest margin, crate | Where | Below 8, crate | Smallest margin, exact polygon rounded once | Below 8, exact polygon rounded once |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 4 | Up to 12 | 2210 | $9.4 \times 10^{4}$ | `*(10)835` | 0 | Not measured | Not measured |
| 5 | Up to 9 | 3536 | $3.5 \times 10^{4}$ | `*88833` | 0 | Not measured | Not measured |
| 6 | Up to 6 | 1505 | $3.5 \times 10^{4}$ | `*665334` | 0 | Not measured | Not measured |
| 7 | Up to 5 | 1300 | $1.8 \times 10^{4}$ | `*5433233` | 0 | Not measured | Not measured |
| 8 | Up to 4 | 498 | $1.4 \times 10^{4}$ | `*44433323` | 0 | Not measured | Not measured |
| 4 | With 1000 | 28 | 68.7 | `*(1000)222` | 0 | 45.8 | 0 |
| 5 | With 1000 | 73 | 17.2 | `*(1000)2332` | 0 | 17.2 | 0 |
| 6 | With 1000 | 208 | 11.5 | `*(1000)22322` | 0 | 11.5 | 0 |
| 7 | With 1000 | 586 | 4.29 | `*(1000)323222` | 9 | 4.29 | 19 |
| 8 | With 1000 | 1720 | 3.44 | `*(1000)3232223` | 197 | 3.44 | 169 |

The residuals of checks 1 and 2 at these signatures are quantized. The value $2.33 \times 10^{-10}$ is $2^{-32}$, the last place of entries of about $2 \times 10^{6}$, and $2.91 \times 10^{-10}$ is $1.25 \times 2^{-32}$, which is the isometry defect of a reflection in a wall far from the centre. Vertex 0 at a corner of order 1000 lies at a distance of about 5.5 from the centre, with a time coordinate of about 124. For five to eight corners, the exact polygon rounded once has the same smallest margins as the crate, so the margins below 8 come from storing the polygon in `f64`, and not from the walk. Section 8.4 states the margin that the design claims from these figures.

The tests "Regular polygon measurements", "Doubled polygon measurements", and "Flat vertex" of Section 12.1 measure the corner angles and the area of DOM-46 on polygons built through private access, not on the sets above. Their set is the regular polygons with $n$ from 5 to 8 corners and every corner of the order $k$, for $k$ in $\{2, 3, 4, 7, 12, 1000\}$, which gives 24 polygons with 156 vertices, and the doubles of the same 24, with 264 vertices. The vertices of the tile of $\{n, 2k\}$ come from equation 24 of [HT], computed in the reference arithmetic of Section 12.2 and rounded once to `f64`, and each wall goes through two rounded vertices. The expected angle is the copies times $\pi/k$, and the expected area is $(n - 2)\pi - n\pi/k$, or twice that for a double. Each bound follows DOM-19:

| Quantity | Set | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Corner angle | The 24 polygons | $10^{-14}$ | $8.269 \times 10^{-16}$ | Vertex 0 of `*2222222` | 12.1 |
| Area | The 24 polygons | $4 \times 10^{-14}$ | $3.736 \times 10^{-15}$ | `*2222222` | 10.7 |
| Corner angle | The 24 doubles | $4 \times 10^{-12}$ | $3.280 \times 10^{-13}$ | Vertex 4 of the double of `*44444444` | 12.2 |
| Area | The 24 doubles | $10^{-11}$ | $5.510 \times 10^{-13}$ | The double of `*33333333` | 18.1 |
| Flat vertex, in the corner frame, against $\pi$ | The 8 vertices with the copies 2 of the doubles of `*22222` to `*22222222` | $4 \times 10^{-14}$ | $2.343 \times 10^{-15}$ | Vertex 7 of the double of `*22222222` | 17.1 |

The largest angle error of the doubles is about 400 times that of the polygons, and the largest area error about 150 times. They come from the image vertices, which are plain `f64` products by $R_1$, and not from the measurement. The test "Flat vertex" also prints, and does not assert, the error of the law of cosines in the triangle of the two neighbours at the same 8 vertices. Its largest error is $6.989 \times 10^{-8}$, at vertex 7 of the double of `*22222222`, which is 70 times the tolerance of check 5. Section 6.12 compares the corner frame with the sum of two angles at the vertices with the copies 2.

Every one of the 24 polygons passes every check. The tests print the largest residuals of checks 5 and 6, which every double also passes:

| Check | Tolerance | Polygons: largest residual, where, margin | Doubles: largest residual, where, margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 5. Angles | $10^{-9}$ | $8.882 \times 10^{-16}$, `*2222222`, $1.13 \times 10^{6}$ | $3.280 \times 10^{-13}$, the double of `*44444444`, 3049.2 |
| 6. Area | $10^{-9}$ | $3.553 \times 10^{-15}$, `*2222222`, $2.81 \times 10^{5}$ | $5.507 \times 10^{-13}$, the double of `*33333333`, 1816.0 |

The test allows the doubles of the regular polygons with every corner of order 1000 to fail check 4 alone, and prints the failure. Their walls are not those of Section 6.10. Section 8.4 states the residuals, refusals, and margins of the doubles that the construction of Section 6.10 gives, over the sweeps of the doubles.

The deterministic companion of the test "Random polygons" counts, for each number of corners, the settings that the guard refuses among its 300, and prints the share. It asserts that no count exceeds twice the count of this table, so that a change that makes the construction less accurate fails it. A program outside the repository repeated the draw with 3000 settings for each number of corners. All three columns draw the lengths uniformly from the range 0.5 to 3.5 and the orders from 2 to 12:

| Corners | Refused by the companion test, of 300 | Refused by the crate, of 3000 | Share refused by the exact polygon rounded once, on the prototype |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 4 | 0 (0%) | 0 (0%) | 0% |
| 5 | 0 (0%) | 0 (0%) | 0% |
| 6 | 0 (0%) | 1 (0.03%) | 0% |
| 7 | 8 (2.7%) | 62 (2.1%) | 2% |
| 8 | 22 (7.3%) | 238 (7.9%) | 8% |

With half of the corners drawn from 900 to 1000, and 300 settings for each number of corners, the crate refused 0.3% with four corners, 1.7% with five, and 10.3% with six. The same draw with seven or eight corners cannot run, because `escher-signature` can reject such a signature, as the test "Random polygons" states.

The test "Cyclic order" runs the named polygons and then the small polygons. It gives each polygon of the named set all its settings, drawn with the seed `0x9e37_79b9_7f4a_7c15`, and every other polygon only its default shape. Each of the 13 named polygons that is also a small polygon, such as `*3333`, therefore runs its settings a second time with new random draws. The test builds 5985 domains, and the guard refuses 68 settings, on the machine of the measurement. Each refused setting is a setting other than the default of a named polygon with a corner of order 1000.

A temporary test of the crate timed the default shapes in a release build, as the mean of 200 repetitions. Every build of a polygon or a double includes its guard, and check 4 follows DOM-49:

| Domain | Time of one build | Time of `verify()` alone |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` | 1.205 ms | 1.179 ms |
| `(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` | 1.205 ms | 1.183 ms |
| `(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` | 0.754 ms | 0.772 ms |
| `*2222222` | 0.0244 ms | 0.0091 ms |
| `2222222` | 0.0281 ms | 0.0110 ms |
| `99999999` | 0.0413 ms | 0.0211 ms |
| `*3333` | 0.0113 ms | 0.0023 ms |
| `3333` | 0.0161 ms | 0.0064 ms |
| `*732` and `732` | Not measured | 0.0013 and 0.0021 ms |

With `f64` arithmetic in check 4, `verify()` of `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` takes 0.0330 ms on the same machine, so check 4 of DOM-49 costs about 36 times as much at an order of 1000. No measurement times a build with every parameter set under DOM-49. A build at the default shape computes the inscribed polygon once, and a build with every parameter set does not compute it (Section 6.6). The times depend on the load of the machine, so compare them only within this table. The test "Checks at the worst cases" also prints the time of one build with its guard, in the debug profile of the tests. Under a load average of about 50, a build of `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` took 26.6 ms and a build of its double 27.4 ms, and in another run 37.3 ms and 46.3 ms. Under a load average of about 15, they took 12.3 ms and 13.2 ms.

The test "Regular polygons" compares with equations 24 and 29 of [HT] (Section 10.4 of the geometry reference), over the seven polygons that its row of Section 12.1 names, with 39 sides and 39 vertices. Each bound follows DOM-19:

| Quantity | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Side against equation 29 of [HT] at $(n, 2p)$ | $10^{-14}$ | $8.358 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 0 of `*22222222` | 12.0 |
| Disk radius of a vertex against equation 24 of [HT] | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.453 \times 10^{-16}$ | Vertex 7 of `*22222222` | 13.8 |

The double-double walk improves on the plain `f64` walk of the prototype, whose sides of `*22222222` were within $1.3 \times 10^{-13}$ of equation 29, more than 100 times the error of the crate. With the defaults unrounded, the prototype also gave the sides of `*4444` within $1.1 \times 10^{-13}$, `*33333333` within $2.8 \times 10^{-12}$, and `*(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)` within $3.3 \times 10^{-9}$. Its vertices lay within $10^{-13}$ of the radius of equation 24 except for `*33333333`, at $2.4 \times 10^{-13}$, and `*(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)`, at $6.9 \times 10^{-10}$. The test holds neither `*33333333` nor `*(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)(12)`.

### 9.8. Accuracy of the Wallpaper Domains

The tests of this section cover the ten domains of Section 6.13 over the grid of Section 8.4: `aspect` in $\{0.125, 0.3, 1, 1.618, 4, 8\}$, and for `o` and `2222` also `skew` in $\{-0.5, -0.3, 0, 0.25, 0.5\}$, with the kites `4*2` and `3*3` once each. The reference of Section 12.2 builds each domain from its closed form in 256-bit arithmetic: the vertices of the table of DOM-50, the walls through them, and the pairings from their definitions as matrices.

On a prototype outside the repository, a reference in 256-bit arithmetic with mpmath gave these largest errors of the stored data against the closed forms, over the grid and the random settings of Section 8.4 (Section 14):

| Quantity | Largest error on the prototype | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | $1.3 \times 10^{-15}$ | The sheared `2222` and `4*2` |
| Covector | $1.8 \times 10^{-15}$ | The sheared `2222` and `4*2` |
| Pairing | $9.0 \times 10^{-16}$ | The sheared `2222` and `4*2` |
| Angle sum of a cycle against $2\pi/m_c$ | $1.8 \times 10^{-15}$ for `o` and $2.7 \times 10^{-15}$ for the sheared `2222` | The worst settings of Section 8.4 |
| Flat vertex against $\pi$ | Exactly 0, because the argument of the arccosine was exactly $-1$ | Every flat vertex of `22*`, `2*22`, and the sheared `2222`, at every setting of the grid of Section 8.4 and at each random setting |
| Covolume of the lattice against the order of the point group times the area | $4.4 \times 10^{-15}$ | The test "Wallpaper certificate" (Section 12.1) |

The report of the prototype states the vertex, covector, and pairing figures as the largest over all ten domains, at the sheared `2222` and at `4*2`, without saying which of the two holds which figure.

The test "Wallpaper accuracy" of Section 12.1 compares each quantity of the tables below with the reference over the domains of this section, asserts each bound, and prints the largest error with its domain, its setting, and its margin. Each bound is the smallest value of the form 1, 2, or 4 times a power of 10 that is at least 8 times the largest error that the crate measures, as TEST-13 of the coding standard and DOM-19 require. The test bounds `2222`, at every value of `skew`, in a set of its own, because most of its largest errors exceed those of every other domain, so one set of bounds over all the domains would hide a loss of accuracy on the others. It bounds the kites `4*2` and `3*3` in a third set, because their covector and pairing errors exceed those of the first set. The tests "Wallpaper accuracy" and "Shear" assert each set apart. These are the three sets:

| Set | Domains | Sides | Cycles | Areas |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| First | `o`, `**`, `xx`, `*x`, `22*`, `22x`, and `2*22` | 282 | 120 | 66 |
| Second | `2222` at every value of `skew` | 180 | 120 | 30 |
| Third | `4*2` and `3*3` | 8 | 6 | 2 |

The first table gives the first set. Its largest errors are all at `o`:

| Quantity | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Largest coordinate error | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.560 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 8, `skew` $-0.5$, vertex 0 | 15.6 |
| Covector | Largest coordinate error | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.455 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 1.618, `skew` $-0.3$, side 1 | 13.7 |
| Pairing | Largest entry error | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.507 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 0.125, `skew` $-0.5$, side 0 | 16.0 |
| Side length | Absolute error | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $3.384 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 0.125, `skew` $-0.3$, side 1 | 11.8 |
| Angle sum of a cycle | Absolute error against $2\pi/m_c$ | $10^{-14}$ | $6.432 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 0.3, `skew` 0.25, cycle 0 | 15.5 |
| Area | Absolute error against 1 | $10^{-15}$ | $1.110 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 0.3, `skew` $-0.3$ | 9.0 |
| Shear vertex | Largest coordinate error of the test "Shear", `o` only | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.220 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 8, `skew` $-0.5$, vertex 0 | 9.0 |
| Shear area | Absolute error of the test "Shear", `o` only | $10^{-15}$ | $1.110 \times 10^{-16}$ | `o`, `aspect` 0.3, `skew` $-0.3$ | 9.0 |

The second table gives the set of `2222` at every value of `skew`. Its area error is 0, so the rule sets no bound for the area. Its bounds of the angle sum and the area are those of the first set:

| Quantity | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Largest coordinate error | $10^{-14}$ | $8.935 \times 10^{-16}$ | `aspect` 8, `skew` $-0.5$, vertex 1 | 11.2 |
| Covector | Largest coordinate error | $10^{-14}$ | $9.513 \times 10^{-16}$ | `aspect` 8, `skew` 0.25, side 2 | 10.5 |
| Pairing | Largest entry error | $10^{-14}$ | $5.121 \times 10^{-16}$ | `aspect` 8, `skew` $-0.5$, side 0 | 19.5 |
| Side length | Absolute error | $10^{-14}$ | $6.374 \times 10^{-16}$ | `aspect` 8, `skew` $-0.5$, side 0 | 15.7 |
| Angle sum of a cycle | Absolute error against $2\pi/m_c$ | $10^{-14}$ | $7.657 \times 10^{-16}$ | `aspect` 4, `skew` $-0.3$, cycle 1 | 13.1 |
| Area | Absolute error against 2 | $10^{-15}$ | 0 | Every setting | Infinite |
| Shear vertex | Largest coordinate error of the test "Shear" | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $4.441 \times 10^{-16}$ | `aspect` 8, `skew` $-0.5$, vertex 1 | 9.0 |
| Shear area | Absolute error of the test "Shear" | $10^{-15}$ | 0 | Every setting | Infinite |

With the bounds of the first set, the vertex, covector, pairing, and side length of `2222` would have the margins 4.5, 2.1, 7.8, and 6.3, and its shear vertex the margin 4.5, below the factor of 8 that this section requires of a bound.

The third table gives the set of the kites, each at its one domain. The kites are not sheared, so the set has no shear rows:

| Quantity | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Largest coordinate error | $10^{-15}$ | $1.090 \times 10^{-16}$ | `3*3`, vertex 1 | 9.2 |
| Covector | Largest coordinate error | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.776 \times 10^{-16}$ | `4*2`, side 2 | 14.4 |
| Pairing | Largest entry error | $10^{-14}$ | $8.882 \times 10^{-16}$ | `4*2`, side 1 | 11.3 |
| Side length | Absolute error | $10^{-15}$ | $1.110 \times 10^{-16}$ | `4*2`, side 2 | 9.0 |
| Angle sum of a cycle | Absolute error against $2\pi/m_c$ | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.449 \times 10^{-16}$ | `4*2`, cycle 1 | 8.2 |
| Area | Absolute error against the area of the reference kite | $10^{-15}$ | $7.757 \times 10^{-17}$ | `3*3` | 12.9 |

With the bounds of the first set, the covector and the pairing of the kites would have the margins 7.2 and 4.5, below the factor of 8 that this section requires of a bound. The bounds of the second and third sets apply to the reference test alone. The tolerances of the checks are those of DOM-9.

Every set also holds these rows:

| Quantity | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Flat vertex | The value of `corner_angles()` against `core::f64::consts::PI` | Exactly equal | Equal at all 78 flat vertices: 2 of `22*`, 1 of `2*22`, and 2 of `2222` at each of their settings | Every flat vertex |
| Centre | Equality of `centre()` with `euclidean::ORIGIN`, or for a kite with `centre()` of the built `*442` or `*632` | Exactly equal | Equal: every domain other than a kite equals `euclidean::ORIGIN`, and each kite equals the centre of its built triangle | Every domain |

`core::f64::consts::PI` differs from $\pi$ by about $1.2 \times 10^{-16}$, so that is the error of a flat vertex against $\pi$.

A temporary run of the test over `xx`, `*x`, and `22x` alone gave these largest errors in the first set, each at `xx` with `aspect` 0.125:

| Quantity | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.254 \times 10^{-16}$ | Vertex 0 | 31.9 |
| Covector | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.254 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 0 | 16.0 |
| Pairing | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.507 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 0 | 16.0 |
| Side length | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.507 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 1 | 16.0 |
| Angle sum of a cycle | $10^{-14}$ | $2.449 \times 10^{-16}$ | Cycle 0 | 40.8 |
| Area | $10^{-15}$ | 0 | Every setting | Infinite |

A temporary run of the test over `22*` and `2*22` alone gave these largest errors in the first set, each at `22*` with `aspect` 0.125. Their 18 flat vertices were exactly `core::f64::consts::PI`:

| Quantity | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.254 \times 10^{-16}$ | Vertex 0 | 31.9 |
| Covector | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $1.254 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 0 | 16.0 |
| Pairing | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.507 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 0 | 16.0 |
| Side length | $4 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.507 \times 10^{-16}$ | Side 2 | 16.0 |
| Angle sum of a cycle | $10^{-14}$ | $2.449 \times 10^{-16}$ | Cycle 1 | 40.8 |

The test "Shear" covers the 24 settings each of `o` and `2222` with `skew` other than 0, and the last two rows of each table give its figures.

The kites keep the vertices, the normals, the covectors, and the pairings of sides 2 and 3 of the kites of `442` and `632` bit for bit, so their errors are those of Section 9.6 except for side 1, whose reflection is computed from its covector (DOM-51).

At `aspect` 1 and 4, and at `skew` 0.25 for `o` and `2222`, every vertex of a rectangular domain, of `o`, and of `2222` is a dyadic rational, and every translation, half-turn, and glide reflection has dyadic entries. The test "Exact wallpaper entries" asserts these vertices and pairings exactly, as TEST-13 of the coding standard allows. It asserts each half-turn at every setting, against the matrix of DOM-51 built from its stored centre, entry for entry. It asserts each glide reflection only at the dyadic settings, against a matrix written out in the test, because a comparison with the product that builds it would only restate the construction.

### 9.9. Accuracy of the Spherical Group Domains

The spherical kites keep the vertices, the normals, the covectors, the centre, and the kept pairings of their doubles bit for bit, so their errors are those of Section 9.6 except for the image mirror side, side 1 for `Low` and side 2 for `High`, whose reflection is computed from its normal (DOM-59). The domains of `nx` keep the vertices, the normals, and the centre of the lune of `*nn` bit for bit, so their errors are those of Section 9.5 except for the pairings, which are exact products of the stored reflections. The domains of `x` and `*` have exact entries, and the whole sphere stores no value but its centre and its area.

The tests of this section run on the spherical group test set of Section 2.1: `3*2`, `*`, `x`, and `1`, the families `n*` and `nx` for $n$ from 2 to 12 and at 1000, and `2*n` for $n$ from 2 to 12 and at 500. The reference of Section 12.2 builds each kite as it builds a double, with the mirror reflections from the reference normals, and each lune of `nx` from the reference values of $\cos(\theta/2)$ and $\sin(\theta/2)$, with the pairings $H R_i$ written componentwise.

These are the figures of the prototype of Section 14. The residuals of `verify()`, over the range of Section 4, from `accuracy.txt` and the sweep of the prototype:

| Range | Failures | Worst residual by check, on the prototype | Smallest margin, on the prototype |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `n*`, $n$ from 1 to 1000 | 0 | Isometries $3.1 \times 10^{-15}$ ($n = 61$), walls $1.8 \times 10^{-15}$, relators $9.4 \times 10^{-14}$ ($n = 960$), angles $2.5 \times 10^{-13}$ ($n = 883$), area $1.3 \times 10^{-13}$ | $4.0 \times 10^{3}$ at $n = 883$ |
| `nx`, $n$ from 1 to 1000 | 0 | All at most $6.7 \times 10^{-16}$ | $1.5 \times 10^{6}$ at $n = 17$ |
| `2*n`, $n$ from 1 to 500 | 0 | Isometries $6.9 \times 10^{-15}$ ($n = 362$), relators $9.4 \times 10^{-14}$ ($n = 480$), angles $2.6 \times 10^{-13}$ ($n = 483$), area $1.3 \times 10^{-13}$ | $3.9 \times 10^{3}$ at $n = 483$ |
| `3*2` | | $1.6 \times 10^{-15}$, $1.6 \times 10^{-15}$, $4.4 \times 10^{-16}$, $9.4 \times 10^{-16}$, $4.4 \times 10^{-16}$, and $1.1 \times 10^{-16}$ for checks 1 to 6 | $6.4 \times 10^{5}$ |
| `*`, `x`, `1` | | 0 for all six checks | Infinite |
| `(1000)*` | | Relators $2.7 \times 10^{-14}$, angles $1.4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $7.1 \times 10^{3}$ |
| `(1000)x` | | At most $2.2 \times 10^{-16}$ | $4.5 \times 10^{6}$ |
| `2*(500)` | | Relators $5.4 \times 10^{-14}$, angles $1.4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $7.0 \times 10^{3}$ |

The rows of `nx` and `(1000)x` do not hold on the crate. Its angles of a lune paired by rotary reflections come from the arccosine of DOM-20, the same angle as the lune of `*nn` bit for bit, so its residuals of checks 5 and 6 are larger than those of the prototype. Section 8.4 gives the figures of the crate.

The ranges of the prototype start at $n = 1$, where `1*` is `*`, `1x` is `x`, and `2*1` is `2*`, the kite of `222` read as `n*` at $n = 2$. The summary of the prototype report gives the smallest margin as $3.92 \times 10^{3}$, and its table gives $3.9 \times 10^{3}$ at `2*(483)`, so the two agree. The report states the margin of each range, and not which check sets it.

The construction error of the prototype against an independent construction in 200-bit arithmetic of mpmath:

| Domain | Normals, on the prototype | Pairings, on the prototype | Vertices, on the prototype |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $n$ up to 12 | At most $4.7 \times 10^{-16}$ | At most $1.2 \times 10^{-15}$ | At most $6.0 \times 10^{-16}$ |
| `(1000)*` | $2.0 \times 10^{-14}$ | $2.7 \times 10^{-14}$ | $2.8 \times 10^{-14}$ |
| `2*(500)` | $2.0 \times 10^{-14}$ | $4.0 \times 10^{-14}$ | $2.8 \times 10^{-14}$ |
| `nx` at every $n$ | At most $6.3 \times 10^{-17}$ | At most $2.2 \times 10^{-16}$ | Exact |

The products of the breadth-first closure of the certificate drift from the exact group elements by up to $2.68 \times 10^{-11}$ at the limits on the prototype, and the folded points by up to $3.0 \times 10^{-11}$. Neither figure is a residual of `verify()`, whose largest residual of check 4 at the limits is $5.4 \times 10^{-14}$.

The prototype recommends these bounds on the largest residual of each check for the tests of this crate, about 4 times its worst measurement:

| Residual | Recommended bound | Worst on the prototype |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Isometries and pairings, checks 1 and 2 | $3 \times 10^{-14}$ | $6.9 \times 10^{-15}$ |
| Walls, check 3 | $10^{-14}$ | $1.8 \times 10^{-15}$ |
| Relators, check 4 | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $9.4 \times 10^{-14}$ |
| Angles and area, checks 5 and 6 | $10^{-12}$ | $2.6 \times 10^{-13}$ |

A bound of about 4 times the worst error leaves a margin below the factor 8 that TEST-13 of the coding standard and DOM-19 require of a bound. So the test "Spherical sweep" asserts the tolerances of DOM-9 with a margin of at least 8, which the prototype meets by more than $3.9 \times 10^{3}$, and prints the largest residual of each check. The bounds of the reference test "Spherical group accuracy" are set by the rule below from the errors that the crate measures, and not from this table.

The test "Spherical group accuracy" of Section 12.1 compares each quantity of this table with the reference over the kites of the spherical group test set, asserts each bound, and prints the largest error with its domain and its margin. Each bound is the smallest value of the form 1, 2, or 4 times a power of 10 that is at least 8 times the largest error that the crate measures, as TEST-13 of the coding standard and DOM-19 require:

| Quantity | Measure | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Largest coordinate error | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $2.785 \times 10^{-14}$ | `(1000)*` | 14.4 |
| Normal | Largest coordinate error | $2 \times 10^{-13}$ | $2.014 \times 10^{-14}$ | `(1000)*` | 9.9 |
| Pairing | Largest entry error | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $4.028 \times 10^{-14}$ | `2*(500)` | 9.9 |
| Side length | Absolute error | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $3.914 \times 10^{-14}$ | `(1000)*` | 10.2 |
| Angle sum of a cycle | Absolute error against $2\pi/m_c$ | $2 \times 10^{-12}$ | $1.419 \times 10^{-13}$ | `2*(500)` | 14.1 |
| Flat vertex | Absolute error of the angle against $\pi$ | $10^{-12}$ | $7.073 \times 10^{-14}$ | `(1000)*` | 14.1 |
| Area | Absolute error against the area of the signature | $4 \times 10^{-14}$ | $4.938 \times 10^{-15}$ | `(1000)*` | 8.1 |
| Centre | Equality with the centre of the built double, or with `spherical::ORIGIN` for the lunes and the whole sphere | Exactly equal, asserted bit for bit | None, because an exact comparison measures no error | Every domain of the set | Not applicable |

The bounds and errors of this table are those of the kites of the spherical group test set, `3*2`, and `n*` and `2*n` for $n$ from 2 to 12 and at their limits. The test compares the centre of each kite bit for bit with the centre of its double.

The test `spherical_lune_groups_are_within_their_bounds_of_the_reference` measures the domains of `nx`, `x`, `*`, and `1` against the same reference with bounds of their own, set by the same rule, over these domains of the spherical group test set: `x`, `*`, `1`, and `nx` for $n$ from 2 to 12 and at 1000. The column "Samples" gives the number of compared values:

| Quantity | Bound | Largest error | Where | Margin | Samples |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex | Exactly equal, asserted bit for bit | 0 | Every domain of the set | Not applicable | 28 |
| Normal | $10^{-15}$ | $6.269 \times 10^{-17}$ | `2x` | 16.0 | 28 |
| Pairing | $2 \times 10^{-15}$ | $2.220 \times 10^{-16}$ | `3x` | 9.0 | 28 |
| Side length | $10^{-15}$ | $1.225 \times 10^{-16}$ | `2x` | 8.2 | 28 |
| Angle sum of a cycle | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $4.075 \times 10^{-14}$ | `(1000)x` | 9.8 | 13 |
| Flat vertex, of `x` and `*` | $10^{-15}$ | $1.225 \times 10^{-16}$ | `x` | 8.2 | 4 |
| Area | $4 \times 10^{-13}$ | $4.075 \times 10^{-14}$ | `(1000)x` | 9.8 | 14 |

The margins come from the printed errors in a debug build. The test also asserts the largest entry of $P_0 P_1 - I$ over `nx` with $n$ from 2 to 12 and at 1000 against the bound $4 \times 10^{-15}$. The entry is $4.441 \times 10^{-16}$, at `5x`, as on the prototype, a margin of 9.0.

The two mirror sides of `n*` lie on one great circle up to rounding (Section 6.16). For `n*` with $n$ from 2 to 12, the test "Kite entries on the sphere" measured a difference between their stored normals of at most $6.418 \times 10^{-16}$, at `(11)*`, and the prototype reported up to $6.4 \times 10^{-16}$. The test asserts no bound on it.

The test asserts these values exactly, as TEST-13 of the coding standard allows for values that the construction stores: the pairings $-I$ of `x` and $\operatorname{diag}(1, 1, -1)$ of `*`, the normals $(0, 0, -1)$ of the lune of order 1, each pairing of `nx` as $H R_i$ of its stored reflection entry for entry, the vertices $(0, \pm 1, 0)$ of every lune, and the area $4\pi$ of the whole sphere as `4.0 * PI`.

## 10. Errors

### 10.1. Structure

DOM-12. The crate MUST implement `Error` as ERR-1 of the coding standard requires, as an opaque struct that carries the operation that failed, with `kind()` returning a `Copy`, `#[non_exhaustive]` `ErrorKind`, because a reviewer checks the error against that rule, and callers match on the kind, never on the text.

`Error` has these private fields:

- `operation`: A `&'static str`, which is `"derive side presentation"` for an error of `side_presentation()` and `"build fundamental domain"` for every other error of this crate.
- `detail`: A private enum with one variant for each cause, which carries the data that the text of Section 10.2 shows: the canonical string of the signature, the order over the limit with the limit that it exceeds, 1000 or the 500 of DOM-63, the number of orders over the limit, the identity of a parameter from which its name and range derive, the error of `escher-geometry` that caused a numerical failure, the refusal of the guard of DOM-44 for a polygon or for a double, the refusal of the guard of DOM-54 for one of the ten domains of Section 6.13, the refusal of the guard of DOM-60 for a spherical group domain, the structural defect of a domain without a side presentation, or the error of `escher-group` that rejected a relator or a side presentation.

`kind()` computes the kind from `detail` with a `match` that has no wildcard arm (TYPE-3 and TYPE-7 of the coding standard). `thiserror` derives the `Display` of the private detail. `Display` and `std::error::Error` for `Error` are written by hand, because a derived `source()` can return only a field of `Error`, not the `escher-geometry` error that one variant of the detail holds, and a separate source field beside the detail would hold one fact in two fields. `Display` for `Error` writes `cannot `, the operation, `: `, and the text of the detail. `source()` returns the `escher-geometry` error for a `Numerical` error that one caused, the `escher-group` error for every `InvalidPairing` error whose relator or presentation `escher-group` rejected, and `None` otherwise, which includes the four structural causes of `InvalidPairing`. No type from a dependency appears in the public surface of the error (ERR-2).

`InvalidPairing` is an error and not a panic for the reason that `Numerical` is: no domain that `build_with` returns produces it, but `side_presentation()` runs on any value of the type, and `escher info` prints its result (DOM-35).

`Numerical` is an error and not a panic. No supported triangle, Euclidean or spherical domain, or double of one is known to produce it, but the construction calls fallible functions of `escher-geometry`, and a panic would stop `escher info`, so returning an error is the sounder option (ERR-5 of the coding standard, Hard Rule 7 of `AGENTS.md`). A polygon produces it when the guard of DOM-44 refuses the shape, which is an expected outcome for settings near the ends of the range (Section 9.7). The double of a polygon produces it when the guard refuses the double, which happens for some signatures with eight orders of which several are large (Section 8.4). One of the ten domains of Section 6.13 produces it when the guard of DOM-54 refuses it, which no setting of the prototype did (Section 8.4). A spherical group domain produces it when the guard of DOM-60 refuses it, which no order of the prototype did (Section 9.9).

### 10.2. Kinds and Text

DOM-13. `Display` for `Error` MUST be `cannot derive side presentation: ` for the kind `InvalidPairing` and `cannot build fundamental domain: ` for every other kind, followed by the text of the detail in this table, because `escher-cli` prints it and its snapshots fix it.

| Kind | When | Detail carries | Text after the prefix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `InvalidPairing` | DOM-35 | The cause: a number of corners other than the number of sides, more than 256 sides, a walk that does not close, a cycle with unequal orders or copies, or the `escher-group` error that rejected a relator or the presentation | `the domain has a different number of corners than of sides`, `the domain has more than 256 sides`, `the side pairings do not close a vertex cycle`, `a vertex cycle joins corners of different orders`, or `the side relators do not form a presentation` |
| `UnsupportedSignature` | The predicate of Section 4 fails, which includes every general non-compact signature (`DEC-19`) | The canonical string | `the signature 4*3 is not supported`, with the canonical string of the signature in place of `4*3` |
| `LimitExceeded` | A supported signature has more than eight orders (DOM-38) | The number of orders | `the signature has 9 orders, more than 8`, with the number of orders in place of `9` |
| `LimitExceeded` | A corner order of a supported signature exceeds 1000 (DOM-3), or the order $n$ of `2*n` exceeds 500 (DOM-63) | The largest order and the limit | `order 1001 is larger than 1000`, with the largest order in place of `1001` and the limit in place of `1000`, such as `order 501 is larger than 500` for `2*(501)` |
| `Numerical` | Step 11 of Section 6.1, step 7 of Section 6.5, step 7 of the triangle or step 4 of the lune of Section 6.7, step 8 of Section 6.8, step 12 of Section 6.10, step 8 of Section 6.13, or step 8 of Section 6.16 | The `escher-geometry` error, if one caused it | `the construction is numerically degenerate` |
| `Numerical` | The guard of DOM-44, step 13 of Section 6.10, for a polygon that fails a check | Nothing | `the shape is too extreme to build accurately` |
| `Numerical` | The guard of DOM-44 for the double of a polygon that fails a check (DOM-47) | Nothing | `the side pairings of the domain cannot be stored accurately enough` |
| `Numerical` | The guard of DOM-54, step 9 of Section 6.13, for one of the ten domains that fails a check | Nothing | `the wallpaper domain fails its verification` |
| `Numerical` | The guard of DOM-60, step 9 of Section 6.16, for a spherical group domain that fails a check | Nothing | `the spherical domain fails its verification` |
| `UnknownParameter` | Step 3 of DOM-18 | The canonical string | `the signature *632 has no shape parameter with one of the given names`, with the canonical string of the signature in place of `*632` |
| `ParameterOutOfRange` | Step 4 of DOM-18 | The identity of the parameter, a private enum from which the name, the minimum, and the maximum of its `ParameterSpec` derive, so that `Error` keeps `Eq` | `the parameter aspect is outside its range of 0.125 to 8`, with the name, the minimum, and the maximum in place of `aspect`, `0.125`, and `8`, such as `the parameter slice1.length is outside its range of 0.5 to 3.5` |

The text `a vertex cycle joins corners of different orders` also covers a cycle whose copies do not sum to 2. The text `the side relators do not form a presentation` always has the `escher-group` error as its `source()`. The texts of `InvalidPairing` name no side and no number other than the fixed limit of 256 sides, because no supported input reaches them, and the tests that reach them through private access assert the kind and the full text.

The texts of `UnknownParameter` and `ParameterOutOfRange` contain no name or value of a setting, because a caller's text may hold any character, and the printed name is that of the `ParameterSpec`. They write each number with the `{}` format of `f64`, which prints the two ends of the range of `aspect` as `0.125` and `8`, and those of `skew` as `-0.5` and `0.5`, so the text of `skew` is `the parameter skew is outside its range of -0.5 to 0.5`.

So `FundamentalDomain::build` on `4*3` returns an `Error` whose `kind()` is `UnsupportedSignature` and whose `Display` text is exactly `cannot build fundamental domain: the signature 4*3 is not supported`. On `*(1001)32` it returns `LimitExceeded` with the text `cannot build fundamental domain: order 1001 is larger than 1000`, and on `*222222222` the text `cannot build fundamental domain: the signature has 9 orders, more than 8`. The text of the guard names no check, because a check residual means nothing to a user who chose a length, and the text is enough for the user to choose a shape nearer the default. A double has no shape to choose, so its text names the cause, the side pairings that `f64` cannot store accurately enough (Section 8.4), and names no remedy. The text of a refused wallpaper domain or spherical group domain names the verification, because no setting or order in range is known to fail it, so a refusal would be a defect of the crate and not a shape to change. The text of a refused spherical group domain is `DEC-60` of the architecture. The private details of the guard carry nothing, so their `source()` is `None`. What `escher-cli` prints for either, and its exit status, are for the command-line design to state.

Every fixed text is in lower case and has no trailing punctuation (ERR-4 of the coding standard). The canonical string contains only printable ASCII (Section 7.3 of the signature design), so the text needs no escaping. The `Display` text does not repeat the text of the `source()` error (ERR-4).

## 11. Resource Limits

The crate limits the number of orders and the largest corner order, which `build_with` checks at step 2 of DOM-18, and the value of each shape parameter, which it checks at step 4. Section 16 states each value and its reason, because Section 5.9 of the architecture places the limits in the Security Considerations.

## 12. Testing

### 12.1. Tests

The unit tests sit beside the code (TEST-1 of the coding standard). The integration tests form one binary, `crates/escher-domain/tests/domain/main.rs`, with the modules `common`, `shared`, `reference`, `triangles`, `regular`, `properties`, `unsupported`, `euclidean`, `parameters`, `spherical`, `rotation`, `presentation`, `polygons`, `wallpaper`, and `spherical_groups` (TEST-10). Its root has a `//!` line. The snapshot tests of the domain summary live in `escher-cli`, and the command-line design specifies them.

The module `common` defines the sets of triples that the tests use:

- The plan triples: $(7, 3, 2)$, $(5, 4, 2)$, $(4, 4, 4)$, $(4, 3, 3)$, $(12, 3, 2)$, and $(1000, 3, 2)$. The plan writes $(3, 3, 4)$, whose canonical signature is `*433`.
- The 24 worst cases: $(995, 983, 970)$, $(994, 991, 970)$, $(997, 996, 970)$, $(997, 980, 973)$, $(992, 990, 979)$, $(995, 987, 976)$, $(928, 4, 3)$, $(999, 7, 6)$, $(998, 4, 4)$, $(1000, 4, 4)$, $(1000, 1000, 2)$, $(1000, 1000, 1000)$, $(868, 4, 4)$, $(923, 6, 5)$, $(998, 981, 961)$, $(998, 998, 958)$, $(997, 931, 906)$, $(992, 991, 975)$, $(989, 976, 974)$, $(978, 977, 971)$, $(985, 985, 971)$, $(973, 972, 964)$, $(979, 979, 971)$, and $(987, 984, 970)$. They include every triple at which the sweeps of Sections 8.4 and 9.3 have a largest value.
- The 30 named triples: the plan triples and the worst cases.
- The orders up to 12: all 269 hyperbolic triples $n_0 \ge n_1 \ge n_2$ with orders up to 12.
- The set of 294 triples: the orders up to 12 and the 25 named triples with an order above 12.
- The cone triples: the same triples read as the orders of rotation signatures, such as `732` for $(7, 3, 2)$, whose domains are kites.

Each test names the item of the field "Tests and acceptance" of Section 5.2 of the plan that it serves, or of Section 5.4, 5.5, 5.6, 5.7, 5.9, or 5.10 of the plan where the item says so. The golden images of the polygons belong to `escher-render`. The tests "Triangles", "Third side", "Checks at the worst cases", "Relator agreement", and the two tests of $\{7, 3\}$ also assert their smallest number of samples, and print each largest value with its triple and its margin. The test harness captures that output for a test that passes:

| Test | Plan item | Kind and place | Asserts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Triangles | Reference arithmetic | Reference test, `triangles` | Vertices, normals, side lengths, the centre, corner angles, and the area are within the bounds of each column of Section 9.3, against the reference of Section 12.2, for the set of 294 triples |
| Third side | Reference arithmetic | Reference test, `triangles` | For the set of 294 triples: in the reference, the side from vertex 0 to vertex 1 of the standard position agrees within $10^{-60}$ with the side opposite vertex 2 by the law of cosines of Section 10.2 of the geometry reference, which checks the placement. The distance from vertex 0 to vertex 1 of the built domain, which the construction never sets, is within the bounds of the side length of Section 9.3 for the orders up to 12 and for the set |
| Checks at the worst cases | Reference arithmetic | Integration test, `triangles` | For the set of 294 triples, and for its cone triples: each check of Section 8.2 passes and `verify().passed()` is true. It prints the largest residual of each check, for the reflection triangles and for the kites apart, which Section 8.4 states, and for the kites the largest residual of check 4 over the cycles of two vertices. The polygon part, `checks_pass_at_the_default_shapes_of_the_polygons_and_their_doubles`, covers the canonical signatures of the 22 named and the 4421 small polygons of Section 9.7, each counted once, at their default shapes, and their doubles, with the counts 4421 and 4430 asserted. It runs `verify()` once on each domain. `build` succeeds and every check passes, except that the double of `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)`, whose smallest margin is below 2, may return `Numerical` with the text of a refused double (Section 8.4). It asserts a margin of at least 8 only inside the claims of Section 8.4: the polygons with orders up to 12, the polygons with four to six corners and a corner of order 1000, and the doubles with orders up to 12. It prints the largest residual of each check for the polygons and for the doubles apart, with the double at the limit printed on its own and left out of the doubles. It also prints the smallest margin inside the claims and overall, and every margin below 8 outside the claims, which Section 8.4 records. Last, it prints the time of one build with its guard of `*(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)(1000)` and of its double, which Section 9.7 records. The companion test `polygons_of_four_to_six_corners_with_a_corner_of_order_1000_keep_their_claimed_margin` builds every polygon of its set of Section 8.4, asserts that each passes every check with a margin of at least 8, and prints the smallest margin for each number of corners, which Section 8.4 records |
| Edge of $\{7, 3\}$ | Numeric checks for $\{7, 3\}$ | Reference test, `regular` | Twice the distance from vertex 1 to vertex 2 of `*732` is within $2 \times 10^{-13}$ of equation 29 of [HT] at $(p, q) = (7, 3)$ |
| Circumradius of $\{7, 3\}$ | Numeric checks for $\{7, 3\}$ | Reference test, `regular` | The length of `hyperbolic::to_disk(hyperbolic::inverse(hyperbolic::translation(V0)) * V1)` of `*732` is within $10^{-13}$ of equation 24 of [HT] at $(p, q) = (7, 3)$ |
